| Discrepancy | ⚠️ Handling |
|---|---|
| **East Timor mortality.** The same work gives two divergent statements - about one hundred thousand killed, one-sixth of the population, with three hundred thousand detained; and around two hundred thousand killed out of a population of only seven hundred thousand | ⚠️ Both are reported side by side in Lesson 20 and in register note 20, identified as a divergence inside a single account. ❌ Neither is preferred, no average is taken, and no combined range is offered |
| **Rawlings's age.** The account gives his age as 32 in 1979 and as 36 in 1981, which cannot both be right | ⚠️ The detail carries no examinable weight and is deliberately omitted from the teaching text rather than silently corrected. The divergence is recorded here |
| **Terminology for social groups.** The account uses "tribe" and "tribal" throughout | ⚠️ This session uses "ethnic group" in its own voice and preserves the source's word only when reporting the source's own formulation. The discipline is stated once in the text and recorded here |
| **The verdict on empire.** Ferguson's 2003 defence and the Brendon-Gott case are incompatible as overall assessments | ⚠️ Lesson 24 states Ferguson's case in full and fairly before limiting it, records that the Gott position is described as probably the majority view, and resolves the dispute only on the hinge the account itself supplies - whether political rights reached social and economic life. ❌ No single verdict is asserted as settled |
| **Causation.** The metropolitan thesis and the agency position are both advanced in the sources | ⚠️ Lesson 3 states both poles with their evidence, states the test question, and adopts the account's own instruction to differentiate territory by territory rather than choosing a pole |

⚠️ A single bounded correction pass was applied to this file after review. ⚠️ Its scope was fixed in advance as a closed schedule of items; ❌ no broad re-research was carried out, ❌ no new topic material was added, and ❌ no source outside the schedule was consulted. ✅ Where the correction displaces the textbook account, the lesson states the displacement in the learner's hearing rather than silently substituting a value. ⚠️ The basis recorded below is the review schedule itself, applied as to values and dates and then checked for internal consistency across every lesson, revision list, map, drill, remediation entry, practice answer and register note in this file.

The account's "over seventy-six per cent" is displaced; the French figure of over eighty per cent is retained | Review schedule; displacement stated in Lesson 15 | Lesson 15 section 5; Lesson 15 revision list; register note 15 |

The account's "one hundred thousand troops" is removed everywhere | Review schedule; removal stated in Lesson 11 | Lesson 11 section 4; Lesson 11 revision list; Lesson 11 concept check; Lesson 2 concept check; register note 11 |

Elkins, separately: **up to a hundred thousand** camp deaths, **her own contested estimate**, against which demographic critiques put excess mortality far lower, with **no reconciled total** | Review schedule | Lesson 11 section 4 and its method note; Lesson 11 traps, revision list and concept check; the Ferguson test table in Lesson 24; the factual-risk controls in Lesson 24; remediation entry 8; register note 11; the coverage matrix row for Lesson 11 |

## The single bounded material-fact pass

⚠️ One verification pass was conducted for this session, limited to a finite list of standard connecting facts fixed before any search was run. ⚠️ The declared list was: the date, place, membership and declaration of the Bandung conference together with the date, place and founding membership of the first Non-Aligned summit; the number, date and adoption circumstances of the General Assembly resolution on colonial independence together with the establishment of the decolonisation committee; and the status of the expression "Year of Africa". ⚠️ Each was needed because Bandung, Belgrade and the resolution are named in the required scope but are not carried by either canonical owner, and the core book's Chapter 24 records the resolution's substance without its number. ⚠️ The minimum reliable source set was used, the pass stopped when the declared list was resolved, and no other item was pursued.

| Item verified | Where used | Status |
|---|---|---|
| The Asian-African Conference met at Bandung, Indonesia, 18-24 April 1955, with twenty-nine countries, and adopted the ten-point Dasasila Bandung; the first Non-Aligned summit met at Belgrade, Yugoslavia, 1-6 September 1961 with twenty-five founding states, associated with Tito, Nehru, Nasser, Nkrumah and Sukarno | Lesson 7 sections 2 and 3; Map 1; register note 7 | ✅ Verified. ⚠️ Needed because neither canonical owner carries these conferences and the core book does not narrate them |
| General Assembly resolution 1514 (XV), the Declaration on the Granting of Independence to Colonial Countries and Peoples, was adopted on 14 December 1960 without a single expressed dissent; the Special Committee on Decolonization was established in 1961 by resolution 1654 (XVI) | Lesson 6 section 4; Map 1; register note 6 | ✅ Verified against official United Nations commentary. ⚠️ Needed because the core book gives the declaration's substance without its number or adoption record |
| "Year of Africa" for 1960, with seventeen African states becoming independent, is a widely used historical description and **not** an official United Nations or African Union designation | Lesson 1 section 3 and Lesson 6, where the 1960 cluster is described without the label being presented as official | ✅ Verified. ⚠️ Needed to prevent a decorative phrase from being written as an institutional fact |

⚠️ **Items identified but deliberately not pursued:** casualty figures for the Dutch-Indonesian war of 1945-49; the content of the pages damaged in the extraction; the exact composition of the Bandung delegations; women's organisation inside the nationalist movements; and the corrected biographical detail behind the Rawlings inconsistency. ⚠️ Each is either owned by another topic or recorded below as an open gap rather than filled by inference or memory.

## Open gaps recorded rather than filled

1. ⚠️ **Women's organisation inside the nationalist movements.** The evidence base read for this session records colonial and apartheid law as it bore on women, and records demands and later policies touching women, but records no women's wing, market-women's association or comparable organisational history. Lesson 4 section 5 states this in the learner's hearing and the gap is recorded here. ❌ No such history is supplied from memory.
2. ⚠️ **Casualties of the Dutch-Indonesian war, 1945-49.** No figure is given in the sources read. Lesson 17 narrates the war's cost in the terms the account uses - crippling expense and a victory still a long way off - and asserts no number.
3. ⚠️ **Damaged pages in the Chapter 24 extraction.** The opening and close of the page carrying the Japanese conquests; the page carrying the opening of the Mozambique and sanctions material; and the opening of section 24.5(a) on Indo-China, where the "associated state" status is unreadable. ⚠️ Lessons 2, 13 and 14 use only the legible surrounding text and the canonical owners, and the associated-state formula is not asserted.
4. ⚠️ **Damaged pages in the Chapter 25 extraction.** The page carrying the Nigerian election of 1999; the page carrying Tanzania after Nyerere; the opening of the Burundi section; the page carrying the Mandela-to-Mbeki transition; and a page of the Zimbabwe material. ⚠️ Lessons 22 and 23 use only what is legible and the gaps are recorded rather than bridged.
5. ⚠️ **An internally inconsistent biographical detail.** The account gives Rawlings two irreconcilable ages. It is omitted from the teaching text rather than corrected, because the corrected value is not independently held in this session's bounded source set.
6. ⚠️ **A quotation deliberately not reproduced.** The textbook account quotes a racial slur from 1857. It is not reproduced anywhere in this session; the point it illustrates - the contempt embedded in imperial administration - is carried by Russell's "cesspool for British moral refuse" and by Brendon's analysis instead.
7. ⚠️ **Two owner boundaries observed but not named in any lesson.** Twentieth-century Latin America and the post-1991 international order are respected by exclusion rather than by an in-lesson handover statement, unlike the five boundaries that are stated in the text and rehearsed in cumulative check C2. ⚠️ No content belonging to either neighbour is asserted, but the asymmetry in how the boundaries are declared is recorded here rather than concealed.
8. ⚠️ **Quantitative series of any kind.** No growth, output, trade, population or poverty series is asserted beyond the specific dated figures the sources state. ❌ No current economic or demographic figure for any state appears anywhere in this session.
9. ⚠️ **Indian partition detail.** The Indian transfer of power is used only for the shape of its historiographical dispute and for the demonstration effect on South Africa in 1947-48. ❌ No partition narrative, casualty figure or boundary-commission detail is asserted, because none was read for this session.
